§ 1. Declaration and findings.-Unjust, unreasonable and oppressive leases and agreements for the payment of rent for commercial space in certain cities having been and being now exacted by landlords from tenants under stress of prevailing conditions accelerated by war, whereby a breakdown has taken place in normal processes of bargaining and freedom of contract has become an illusory concept, and whereby there have come into existence conditions threatening to obstruct the production and distribution of essential civilian commodities, and to cause inflation, and all of the foregoing situations and conditions being a threat 5

COMMERCIAL SPACE RENT LAW 

to essential civilian activities, and to the public safety, health, and general welfare of the people of the state of New York, it is hereby declared that a public emergency exists without promise of relief so long as conditions brought about by war continue, and that action by the legislature is imperative and will not permit of delay. It is hereby found by the legislature that for the duration of such emergency, the establishment of a maximum rent for commercial space at a level of fifteen per centum above rents charged on March first, nineteen hundred forty-three, or at a level otherwise determined as provided herein, will curb the evils arising from such emergency and will accomplish the purposes hereby sought to be achieved. This act is declared to be a measure designed to protect and promote the public health, safety and general welfare, to aid essential civilian activities, and to prevent inflation, and is made necessary by an existing public emergency. § 2. Definitions.-Unless expressly otherwise provided, whenever used in this act, the following terms shall mean or include: (a) "Commercial space." Any space, including space used as a parking lot for motor vehicles, used or occupied for commercial purposes in any city other than space used or occupied as a store or office. (b) "Commercial purposes." The manufacture, sale, resale, processing, reprocessing, displaying, storing, handling, garaging or distribution of personal property. (c) "Store." Any space predominantly used or occupied by a tenant for the sale of personal property or the rendition of services in the ordinary course of business, provided that such rental space shall also include space for the conduct of such business on the street floor or level. (d) "Office." Any space used or occupied under a lease

or rental agreement as a separate business unit in any building which space is predominantly used or occupied for other than commercial purposes. (e) "Emergency rent." The rent reserved or payable under any lease, agreement or tenancy of commercial space in force on March first, nineteen hundred forty-three, plus fifty per centum of such rent, provided only that it shall be not less than the rent payable on July first, nineteen hundred fifty-seven; provided, however, that if the commercial space was not used or occupied on March first, nineteen hundred forty-three, for commercial purposes, the emergency rent shall be the reasonable rent therefor as of such date, plus fifty per centum thereof, to be fixed by agreement, by arbitration, or by the supreme court upon the basis of the rent charged on such date for the most nearly comparable commercial space in the same building, or other rental area, or other satisfactory evidence; provided further that if the tenant's lease, agreement or tenancy of commercial space was made, executed or entered into prior to 

June first, nineteen hundred thirty-nine and the same or any renewal or extension thereof has been continuously in force since that date, a rent, exceeding in amount the emergency rent, may be fixed by the tenant and the landlord by written agreement in accordance with the provisions herein below contained, or upon the landlord's application shall be fixed by the supreme court, in which case the rent to be so fixed by the supreme court shall be equal to the emergency rent chargeable for the most nearly comparable commercial space in the same building or other rental area under any lease, agreement or tenancy thereof made, executed or entered into on or after June first, nineteen hundrd* thirty-nine but prior to March first, nineteen hundred forty-three. • So in original

(f) "Personal property." All goods and chattels personal other than things in action, money, insurance contracts, stocks, bonds, mortgages, notes or other obligations for the payment of money. (g) "Sale." A sale or offering for sale of personal property by sample or otherwise, or a sale of personal property on consignment. (h) "Landlord." An owner, lessor, sublessor, receiver, trustee, executor, assignee or other person receiving or entitled to receive rent for the use or occupancy of the whole or a part of any part of any commercial space. (i) "Tenant." A lessee, sublessee, licensee, or other person entitled to the possession or to the use or occupancy of the whole or a part of any commercial space. (j) "Rent." The consideration, including any bonus, benefit, or gratuity, charged or received by the landlord, his agents, or other representatives for the use or occupancy of the whole or any part of any commercial space. 

(k) "Services." Repairs, decorating and maintenance, the furnishing of light, heat, steam, hot and cold water, telephone, elevator service, cleaning service, linen service, janitor service, the removal of refuse and any other facility or privilege connected with and furnished by the landlord for the use or occupancy of the commercial space. (1) "Person." An individual, corporation, partnership, association, or any other organized group of individuals or the legal successor or representative of any of the foregoing. (m) "Supreme court." A special term of the supreme court to be held in the county in which the commercial space is located. (n) “City.” A city having more than one million inhabitants.

§ 3. Statement of emergency rent.-From and after the twenty-fourth day of January, nineteen hundred forty-five, and during the continuance of the emergency as defined in section eighteen, any rent which exceeds the emergency rent shall be presumed to be unjust, unreasonable and oppressive. Every landlord, within twenty days after the date this section becomes law, shall furnish each tenant with an accurate statement of the amount of his emergency rent, and in the case of commercial space not used or occupied on March first, nineteen hundred forty-three, such statement shall be furnished within twenty days after such rent shall be fixed or determined pursuant to subdivision (e) of section two hereof. In the case of a tenant taking possession of space on or after the date this section as amended, takes effect, such statement shall be furnished within twenty days after the effective date of such new tenant's lease, rental agreement or tenancy. If a landlord shall fail, refuse or neglect to furnish any tenant with such statement within the time specified, no rent accruing shall be collectible by such landlord during the period he is in default. Acceptance by a landlord of payment of the emergency rent by a tenant shall not be construed to be a waiver by such landlord of his right to seek increased rent from such tenant as hereinafter provided. § 4. Increased rent; arbitration; court proceedings; agreement; subtenants.-1. A rent, exceeding in amount the emergency rent, may within the limitations specified by this section, be fixed by arbitration or by the supreme court. 

The rent to be so fixed shall be a reasonable rent based on the fair rental value of the tenant's commercial space as of the date the application to the supreme court or submission to arbitration is made and the landlord shall be entitled to receive rent so fixed dating from the time of such applica-

tion or submission. In the determination of the amount of such reasonable rent: (a) due consideration shall be given to the cost of maintenance and operation of the entire property (including land and building or other rental area in which such commercial space is located) including amounts paid for taxes assessed against such property, and to the kind, quality and quantity of services furnished, but excluding amortization or interest paid or accrued on any incumbrances thereon; (b) such rent shall be fixed in such a manner that it shall not exceed a fair and reasonable proportion of the gross rentals from all the commercial space in the entire building or other rental area, giving due consideration to the amount and character of the commercial space used or occupied by such tenant, provided, however, that the gross rentals from all such commercial space shall not exceed a fair and reasonable proportion of the gross rentals from the entire building or other rental area. 

A net annual return of eight per centum on the fair value of the entire property including the land shall be presumed to be a reasonable return. The assessed valuation of the entire property, including land and building, as shown by the latest completed assessment-roll of the city, shall be presumed to be the fair value of the premises, but other lawful evidence of the fair value may be offered and received. In any proceeding or arbitration under this section, the landlord within five days after written demand by the tenant or within such time as the arbitrators or the supreme court upon good cause shown may determine, shall serve upon the tenant a verified bill of particulars, setting forth the gross income derived from the entire building or other rental area during the preceding year, the names and addresses of all tenants, the rental charged each tenant and how payable, the consideration paid by the landlord for the entire property including the land, if he be the owner 10

thereof, or if he be a lessee the name and address of the lessor and the rent agreed to be paid; the assessed valuation of the property as shown by the latest completed assessment-roll of the city, separately showing the amount of the assessment on the building and the amount of the assessment on the land; the cost of maintenance and operation of the building or other rental area during the preceding year, the kind, quality and quantity of services furnished during such year; and such other facts as the landlord claims affect the net income of the entire building or other rental area, or the reasonableness of the rent to be charged. Issue shall not be deemed to be joined in any proceeding under this section until the bill of particulars is served upon the tenant. Upon failure to serve the bill of particulars upon the tenant within the time limited, the proceeding or arbitration shall be dismissed upon motion of the tenant. At any stage of the proceeding the tenant and the landlord may enter into a written stipulation that the fair rental value of the commercial space occupied by the tenant is the amount stated in such stipulation and consenting that the court so determine, and the court, upon such stipulation, may make its determination of such fair rental value in the amount so stated. As a condition precedent to being heard in any proceeding or arbitration under this section, the tenant must pay to the landlord the emergency rent on each date when the rent is due under the terms of his lease, rental agreement or tenancy. Nothing in this act shall be construed to require submission of any controversy to arbitration. 

2. A rent, exceeding the amount of the emergency rent, may likewise be fixed by the supreme court as provided in this subdivision. In any such additional alternative proceeding to so fix rents in a building or other rental area, the court shall first determine the basic return on the 11

property as in subdivision one of this section prescribed. 

A net annual return of eight per centum on the fair value of the entire property including the land shall be presumed to be the basic return. When the amount of such basic return has been determined, the court shall then determine their fair rental value per square foot of the rentable commercial space in such building or other rental area, taking into account the character and location of such space, by dividing the amount of the total net rentable space into the total of the basic return found as aforesaid. At any stage of the proceeding the tenant and the landlord may enter into a written stipulation that the fair rental value per square foot of the commercial space occupied by the tenant is the amount stated in such stipulation and consenting that the court so determine, and the court, upon such stipulation, may make its determination of such fair rental value in the amount so stated. Thereafter the landlord shall be entitled to receive rent, dating from the time the application was made, at the rate per square foot so determined and the court shall fix at such rate the rent of any tenant occupying commercial space in such building. or other rental area and who is paying rent at a rate per square foot less than the fair rental value so fixed; provided, however, that the rental increase which the landlord shall be entitled to receive and collect during the first twelve months after the date of the commencement of the proceeding shall be limited to fifteen per centum of the rent being paid by the tenant at the date of such commencement and provided further that the order to be entered therein shall provide that the landlord is entitled to receive and collect a further additional increase during each succeeding twelve month period, not exceeding fifteen per centum of the amount paid by the tenant at the date of the commencement of the proceeding until the rent shall equal 12

the rental value as determined by the court in such proceedings. Nothing in this subdivision contained shall operate to affect any rent payable under any unexpired lease or agreement. Upon the expiration of any lease or agreement the rent payable thereunder shall be the emergency rent if such rent be equal to or in excess of the fair rental value per square foot as in this subdivision determined and fixed. 

3. In any case, the tenant and the landlord may fix a reasonable rent by written agreement, signed by both the tenant and the landlord, provided that such written agreement contains a statement setting forth: (i) the amount of the emergency rent for the tenant's space; (ii) a statement that the landlord has advised the tenant prior to the making of such agreement of his right to continue payment of the emergency rent until modified by arbitration or by the supreme court pursuant to section four of this act; and (iii) a statement that the tenant, within sixty days after the date of the execution of such agreement, may cancel such agreement by notice to the landlord by registered mail enclosed in a securely sealed post-paid wrapper, addressed to the other party at his last known address and requiring a return receipt. 

4. At the option of the landlord, (a) any tenant who shall sublet twenty per centum or more of the commercial space occupied by him shall pay to the landlord each month the gross rents receivable by such tenant from all subtenants, less the reasonable value of the services applied by the tenant to such subtenants, plus the emergency rent allocable to the commercial space retained by the tenant for his own use; or (b) any person who shall occupy or use commercial space as sublessee or subtenant of a tenant 13

shall be subject to the obligations and liabilities of such tenant as tenant in such space and the landlord shall be entitled to receive from such sublessee or subtenant or successor thereof the rent which such tenant is or was obligated to pay the landlord as the emergency rent of the premises, except that any such sublessee or subtenant who shall occupy less than the entire space of the tenant shall pay rent to the landlord in the proportion which the space occupied or used by him bears to the entire rent which the tenant is or was obligated to pay to the landlord, less the reasonable value of the services furnished by the landlord to other occupants of such space, if any; or (c) after a tenant has vacated such space the landlord may elect to offer the entire space which was demised to the tenant to all sublessees or subtenants, if such sublessees or subtenants became such with the consent or approval of the landlord, at a rent reserved or payable as emergency rent by the tenant at the time he vacated such space, and, upon the failure of any such sublessee or subtenant to execute such a lease for a period of one year the landlord shall be entitled to remove such sublessee or subtenant and in such event the issuance of a final order in a summary proceeding shall be stayed for a period of not more than sixty days, but, in the event such sublessees or subtenants became such without the consent or approval of the landlord, the provisions of section thirteen of this act relating to space vacated by a tenant shall apply in all respects to such space.

Any tenant who shall sublet twenty per centum or more of the commercial space occupied by him shall furnish the landlord, upon the written request of the landlord, a written and verified statement containing the following information with respect to each of such subtenants, viz: the name and address, the amount of space occupied, the emergency 14

COMMERCIAL SPACE RENT Law rent, and the terms of the lease, rental agreement or tenancy under which occupied; and, if such space is occupied under a written lease or rental agreement, a true copy thereof shall be appended to such statement. Such request by the landlord shall be made within thirty days prior to the date of expiration of the lease or rental agreement of the tenant, or at any time thereafter while such tenant remains in possession, and such statement shall be furnished by the tenant within twenty days after the making of such request. 

5. Any landlord who shall wilfully demand or accept a rent in excess of the emergency rent, or a rent fixed pursuant to section four of this act, shall forfeit one succeeding month's rent. 

6. The rent fixed by arbitration or by the supreme court or the rent reserved or payable under any such written agreement shall be the emergency rent for such space until and unless a new rent shall be fixed by arbitration or the supreme court or the tenant or a successor tenant and the landlord shall make a new agreement. § 5. Increased rent prohibited. Nothing contained in this act shall authorize a landlord to seek, demand or receive increased rent, (a) under any lease or rental agreement made prior to January twenty-fourth, nineteen hundred forty-five, wherein the rent reserve does not exceed the emergency rent; or (b) under any lease or rental agreement made on or after such date wherein the rent reserved is less than the emergency rent. § 6. Eviction proceedings; petition.-No proceeding shall be instituted or maintained, during the continuance of the emergency declared by this act, to recover possession of any commercial space for or on account of a default in payment of rent, unless the petitioner shall allege in the 15

petition and prove to the satisfaction of the court: (a) that the rent charged is not greater than the emergency rent for such commercial space or such greater rent therefor as may have been fixed pursuant to section four of this act, and where a separate charge is made for furnishing live steam, that such charge does not exceed the charge made by a public utility for the same service; and (b) that the services furnished by the landlord to the tenant have not been unreasonably diminished since the effective date of the existing lease, rental agreement or tenancy. To the extent that the rent charged is in excess of the emergency rent, or such greater rent as may have been fixed pursuant to section four of this act, the tenant may interpose the defense that the rent charged is unjust, unreasonable and oppressive and, if such defense be interposed, the rent charged, to the extent of such excess, shall be uncollectible. § 7. Actions to recover rent. (a) In any action to recover rent for commercial space accruing during the period of the emergency, it shall be a defense that such rent is unjust, unreasonable and oppressive if such rent is in excess of the emergency rent or any rent which may be fixed pursuant to section four of this act or that a separate charge is made for furnishing live steam which charge exceeds the charge made by a public utility for the same service, and to the extent of such excess the same shall be uncollectible. (b) The tenant may interpose the defense that the rental value of the commercial space has been reduced by reason of an unreasonable diminution of service, and to the extent that the court shall find that such services have been so diminished, the value thereof shall be allowed in reduction of the rent charged and shall be uncollectible; or, in the alternative, the tenant shall be entitled to a cause of action to recover a proportionate amount of the rent 16

paid, or may apply to the court for injunctive relief and, in such an action, the tenant shall be entitled to prove and collect damages. § 8. Eviction, grounds for. So long as the tenant continues to pay the rent to which the landlord is entitled, under the provisions of this act, no tenant shall be removed from any commercial space, by action or proceeding to evict or to recover possession, by exclusion from possession, or otherwise, nor shall any person attempt such removal or exclusion from possession, notwithstanding that such tenant has no lease or that his lease or other rental agreement has expired or otherwise terminated, and notwithstanding the issuance of any order to dispossess, warrant or process prior to January twenty-fourth, nineteen hundred forty-five, and regardless of any contract, lease, agreement or obligation heretofore or hereafter entered into which is inconsistent with any of the provisions of this act, unless: (a) The tenant has unreasonably refused the landlord, or a purchaser of the premises under a contract of sale with the landlord, access to any part of the commercial space for the purpose of inspection, surveying or the making of borings or subsurface examinations which do not unduly interfere with the business of the tenant, or of showing such space to a prospective purchaser, mortgagee, or prospective mortgagee, or other person having a legitimate interest therein; provided, however, that such refusal shall not be ground for removal or eviction if such inspection or showing of the commercial space is contrary to the provisions of the tenant's lease or other rental agreement; or (b) The tenant (1) has violated a substantial obligation of his lease, rental agreement or tenancy, other than an obligation to pay rent, and has continued or failed to cure 

—2 

17

such violation after written notice by the landlord that the violation cease, or (2) is committing or permitting a nuisance or is using or permitting a use of the commercial space for illegal purposes, or (3) has failed or refused to furnish the landlord the statement required as provided in subdivision four of section four; or (c) The landlord seeks in good faith to recover possession for the immediate purpose of demolishing or reconstructing the building or other rental area or completing said demolition or reconstruction with the intention of constructing a new building or substantially reconstructing the building, and the plans for such construction or reconstruction have been approved by the proper authorities, if such approval is required by law. The words "reconstructing" or "reconstruction" as used in this subdivision (c) shall be limited to substantial structural changes involving alterations affecting at least fifty per centum of the entire rentable space thereof or which increases the rentable space thereof by at least twenty per centum. If the landlord shall fail to start the demolition or reconstruction of the building or other rental area within ninety days after the removal of the tenant, or if after having commenced the demolition or reconstruction of such building or other rental area shall fail or neglect to prosecute the work with reasonable diligence, he shall, unless for good cause shown, be liable to the tenant for all damage sustained on account of such removal; provided, however, that if the building contains both commercial and dwelling space, it shall be a defense to an action for damage if the conduct of the landlord does not constitute a violation of any local law providing penalties for failure to demolish or to comply with a certificate of eviction issued under the emergency housing rent control law. In addition to any other damage sustained, the cost 

18

of removal of plant and property shall be a lawful measure of damage; or (cc) The landlord seeks in good faith to recover possession for the immediate purpose of demolishing the building or other rental area to recover possession of land which is part of an entire plot with the intention of constructing a new building, which construction cannot be begun or is prohibited or the approval of the plans therefor cannot be obtained because of priorities provided by the defense production act of nineteen hundred fifty, as amended, and regulations promulgated and orders issued thereunder. 

Until such approval can be obtained or construction begun, the landlord shall be authorized to use the land upon which such building stood prior to demolition or such other rental area or other parts of such plot as a parking lot for motor vehicles; or (ccc) The landlord seeks in good faith to recover possession for the immediate purpose of demolishing the building or other rental area with the intention of creating and constructing a parking lot for motor vehicles, where the assessed value of the improvements is less than sixty per centum of the assessed value of the land and improvements thereon. If the landlord shall fail to start demolition of the improvements within ninety days after the removal of the tenant or tenants or after having commenced demolition shall fail or neglect to prosecute the work with reasonable diligence, he shall, unless for good cause shown, be liable to the tenant or tenants for all damage sustained on account of such removal. In addition to any other damage sustained the cost of removal of plant and property shall be a lawful measure of damage. (d) (1) The landlord owned or acquired an enforceable right to buy or take possession of the building or other rental area on or before January twenty-fourth, nineteen 19

hundred forty-five and seeks in good faith to recover possession of the commercial space for his immediate and personal use; or possession is sought by a person who acquires title to the building or other rental area subsequent to such date or to give possession to a person who subsequent to such date acquires at least ninety per centum of the stock of a corporation which owns property in which commercial space is located, and who likewise seeks in good faith to recover possession of the commercial space for his immediate and personal use; or possession is sought by such landlord or by such person to give possession to another person who is a subsidiary corporation at least ninety per centum of whose stock is owned by such landlord or by such person, and such landlord or such person seeks in good faith to recover possession of the commercial space for the immediate and personal use of such subsidiary corporation; provided, however, that in any event specified in this paragraph, such landlord or such person who acquired title after such date shall have an equity in the property of not less than twenty per centum of the purchase price or shall have paid in cash on the date of purchase of the stock not less than twenty per centum of the sum total of the encumbrances on such property plus the consideration paid for such stock; and, provided, further, that nothing in this subdivision contained shall authorize the dispossession of a tenant during the term of his lease by such landlord or such person unless by the terms of the lease the privilege is reserved to terminate the lease; and provided further that no such landlord or such person shall be entitled to invoke the provisions of this paragraph unless such landlord, person or corporation shall possess an interest of not less than fifty per centum of the whole investment in the business which it is proposed to carry on in such space; or 20

(2) Possession is sought by a person who is the lessee or sub-lessee of an entire building for a term of twenty-one years or more who seeks in good faith to obtain possession of the commercial space for his immediate and personal use, or possession is sought by such lessee or sub-lessee to give possession to another person who is a subsidiary corporation at least ninety per centum of whose stock is owned by such lessee or sub-lessee, and such lessee or sub-lessee seeks in good faith to recover possession of the commercial space for the immediate and personal use of such subsidiary corporation; or possession is sought by the landlord who seeks in good faith to obtain possession of the business space for the immediate and personal use of such lessee or sublessee or of such subsidiary corporation; or (2-a) Possession is sought by a person who is the lessee or sublessee under a lease for a term of five years or more who seeks in good faith to obtain possession of commercial space, in addition to the space already occupied by him in such building, for his immediate and personal use, or possession is sought by such lessee or sub-lessee to give possession to another person who is a subsidiary corporation at least ninety per centum of whose stock is owned by such lessee or sub-lessee, and such lessee or sub-lessee seeks in good faith to recover possession of the commercial space for the immediate and personal use of such subsidiary corporation; or (3) Possession is sought by a landlord of a rental area upon which no permanent buildings are erected or which is used as a parking lot for motor vehicles, for the purpose of erecting a new building in case such landlord acquired title to such rental area subsequent to such date; 

If such landlord or such person shall fail, after thirty days subsequent to dispossessing a tenant under the provisions of this subdivision, to occupy such space and actively 21

to conduct such business therein, or if such landlord acquired possession of a rental area for the purpose of erecting a new building shall fail to file for approval a plan to construct such multiple dwelling with the appropriate municipal agency after thirty days subsequent to dispossessing such tenant or fails diligently to perform the acts necessary to commence construction, or if such landlord or such person shall lease or rent such space to or permit occupancy thereof by a third person within a period of one year after such dispossession, he shall be liable to the tenant for all damages sustained on account of such removal, provided, however, that if the making of alterations shall prevent the active conduct of such business therein, such period of thirty days shall begin to run after the completion of such alterations. In addition to any other damages, the cost of removal of plant and property shall be a lawful measure of damage. In any case where such landlord or lessee during such year shall permit the making of alterations within such space for a tenant who shall become entitled to possession after the termination of such period of one year, such liability for damages on account of dispossession shall not apply; or (e) The tenant whose lease or rental agreement has expired or shall hereafter expire during the continuance of the emergency declared by this act has refused, subsequent to January twenty-fourth, nineteen hundred fortyfive, to execute, upon demand of the landlord, a renewal of the prior lease or rental agreement for a further term of like duration or for such shorter term as the landlord may elect, on substantially similar terms and conditions as are contained in such prior lease or rental agreement, provided that the terms and conditions of such renewed lease or rental agreement are not inconsistent with any of the provisions of this act, and provided that such demand be trans- 22

mitted by the landlord to the tenant by registered mail, and provided further that the rent reserved in such renewed lease or rental agreement may be any amount not in excess of the emergency rent for such tenant's commercial space. 

In the case of an unexpired lease, the tenant shall be required to agree to execute such renewal not more than twelve months and not less than six months prior to the expiration date of the lease, and in the case of an expired lease, within thirty days subsequent to the receipt of such demand. The landlord shall not be entitled to seek a fixation of rent pursuant to section four of this act where the rent reserved in any such renewed lease or rental agreement is less than the emergency rent; or (f) The tenant occupies commercial space in a building or other rental area required to be demolished in order to carry out a housing or rehabilitation project instituted under the public housing law, the redevelopment companies law, or section eighty-four of the insurance law; or (g) The tenant, being in possession under an expired. lease or tenancy or as a monthly or statutory tenant, agrees in writing, or notifies the landlord in writing of his intention, to terminate his occupancy of the premises on a date certain in the future, or a tenant in possession under a lease not yet expired agrees in writing by agreement executed not less three months after the commencement of the term thereof, to terminate his occupancy at any time before or after expiration of the term of such lease. Any tenant, being in possession under an expired lease or tenancy or as a monthly or statutory tenant, who vacates premises without giving the landlord at least two months' written notice by registered mail of his intention to vacate shall be liable to the landlord for an additional two months' rent. (gg) (1) The landlord on or after June first, nineteen hundred sixty-one, offers to enter into a lease with a tenant 23

in possession of a store under an expired lease or tenancy or as a monthly or statutory tenant for a term of at least two years to commence on the first day of the month next succeeding the twentieth day after the mailing of such offer as hereinafter provided except that any offer heretofore made for a lesser term shall continue to be valid if such offer was for a term which was permitted hereunder at the time such offer was transmitted, at a rental in the same amount as the emergency rent with respect to the store occupied by the tenant and otherwise on substantially similar terms and conditions as existing with respect to such tenancy not inconsistent with any of the provisions of this act, and such offer is transmitted by the landlord to the tenant by registered mail, and such tenant fails, neglects or refuses to execute such lease within such period of twenty days, and a further period of six months elapses subsequent to such failure, neglect or refusal of the tenant; or (2) The landlord on or after June first, nineteen hundred sixty-one, offers to enter into a lease with a tenant in possession of any commercial space with respect to which the emergency rent is twelve hundred fifty dollars or more per year, other than a store, under an expired lease or tenancy or as a monthly or statutory tenant, for a term of at least two years to commence on the first day of the month next succeeding the twentieth day after the mailing of such offer as hereinafter provided, at a rental in the same amount as the emergency rent with respect to the commercial space occupied by the tenant and otherwise on substantially similar terms and conditions as existing with respect to such tenancy not inconsistent with the provisions of this act, and such offer is transmitted by the landlord to the tenant by registered mail, and such tenant fails, neglects or refuses to execute such lease within such period of twenty days, and a 24

further period of six months elapses subsequent to such failure, neglect or refusal of the tenant; or (h) On or before January twenty-fourth, nineteen hundred forty-five: (1) The landlord acquired an enforceable right to take possession of the entire building or other rental area for a term of not less than ten years; (2) a person owned and owns, at least ninety per centum of the stock of a corporation which owned and owns the entire premises or which was the lessee of the entire building or other rental area for a term of not less than ten years; or (3) a corporation at least ninety per centum of whose stock was owned and is owned by a person, or a whollyowned subsidiary corporation of such person, which person owned and owns the entire premises or who was the lessee of the entire building or other rental area for a term of not less than ten years or who owned all of the stock of another subsidiary corporation which was the lessee of the entire building or other rental area for a term of not less than ten years; or (4) a corporation leased and leases commercial space from a person who owned and owns or who was the lessee of the entire building for a term of not less than ten years and who also owned and owns at least ninety per centum of the stock of such corporation; and such landlord, person or corporation seeks in good faith to recover possession of the commercial space for his or its own immediate and personal use; provided, however, that nothing in this subdivision contained shall authorize the dispossession of a tenant during the term of his lease by such landlord, person or corporation, unless by the terms of the lease, the privilege is reserved to terminate the lease for reasons which would not preclude the remedy afforded under this subdivision; or 25

(5) The landlord in writing executed and delivered a lease of the entire building or other rental area for a term of not less than ten years and the tenant named in said lease has demanded in writing from the landlord possession of such building or other rental area for his own and immediate use under the terms of said lease. If the landlord, person or corporation shall fail, after thirty days subsequent to dispossessing a tenant under the provisions of this subdivision, to occupy such space and actively to conduct such business therein, or if the landlord, person or corporation shall lease or rent such space to or permit occupancy thereof by a third person within a period of one year after such dispossession, he or it shall be liable to the tenant for all damages sustained on account of such removal. In addition to any other damage, the cost of removal of plant and property shall be a lawful measure of damage. (i) an unincorporated labor organization which owns at least ninety per centum of the stock of a corporation owning the entire premises seeks in good faith to recover possession of the commercial space for its own immediate and personal use; provided, however, that nothing in this subdivision contained shall authorize the dispossession of a tenant during the term of his lease by such unincorporated labor organization, unless by the terms of the lease, the privilege is reserved to terminate the lease for reasons which would not preclude the remedy afforded under this subdivision. If the unincorporated labor organization shall fail, after thirty days subsequent to dispossessing a tenant under the provisions of this subdivision, to occupy such space and actively to conduct such business therein, or if the unincorporated labor organization shall lease or rent such space to or permit occupancy thereof by a third person within a period of one year after such dispossession, it shall be liable to the tenant for all damages sustained 26

on account of such removal. In addition to any other damage, the cost of removal of plant and property shall be a lawful measure of damage. (j) one or more corporations or unincorporated organizations or associations, each organized exclusively for religious, charitable, health, hospital, educational or scientific purposes, or for two or more such purposes, and not for any pecuniary profit for itself or any officer, member or employee thereof, except reasonable compensation for services in effecting one or more of such purposes, which own the entire premises or are members of a membership corporation owning the entire premises or own at least ninety per centum of the stock of a stock corporation owning the entire premises, seek in good faith to recover possession of the commercial space for the immediate and personal use of themselves or any of them; provided, however, that nothing in this subdivision contained shall authorize the dispossession of a tenant during the term of his lease by any such corporation or corporations or unincorporated organization or organizations or association or associations, unless by the terms of the lease the privilege is reserved to terminate the lease for reasons which would not preclude the remedy afforded under this subdivision. If none of the corporations or unincorporated organizations or associations shall, within thirty days subsequent to dispossessing a tenant under the provisions of this subdivision, occupy such space and actively conduct such business therein, or if the corporation or corporations or unincorporated organization or organizations or association or associations shall lease or rent such space to or permit occupancy thereof by a third person within a period of one year after such dispossession, it or they shall be liable to the tenant for all damages sustained on account of such removal. In addition to 27

any other damage, the cost of removal of plant and property shall be a lawful measure of damage. (k) the landlord, subject to the following conditions, receives and accepts a bona fide offer to enter into a lease with a prospective tenant for any store occupied by one tenant which lease provides for a rental of twelve hundred fifty dollars per annum or more, for a term of not less than ten years, non-cancellable except for violation of any term or obligation of such lease, and the landlord offers to execute a lease containing the same terms and conditions, with the tenant in possession and delivers to the tenant in possession a copy of such lease with the prospective tenant, and such tenant fails, neglects or refuses to execute such a lease within thirty days after tender thereof to such tenant; provided, however, that in the case of such a lease with a prospective tenant for a store or stores occupied by more than one tenant, and in which lease the rent reserved is ten thousand dollars per annum or more, the landlord shall not be required to offer to execute or to tender the lease to any such tenant; and provided, further, that nothing in this subdivision contained shall authorize the dispossession of a tenant during the term of his lease by such landlord unless, by the terms of the lease, the privilege is reserved to terminate the lease for reasons which would not preclude the remedy afforded under this subdivision. If such prospective tenant shall fail, after thirty days subsequent to such dispossession, to occupy such store, or if the landlord shall lease or rent such store to or permit occupancy thereof by a third person within a period of one year after such dispossession, the landlord shall be liable to the tenant so dispossessed for all damages sustained on account of such removal, provided, however, that if the making of alterations shall prevent the active conduct of such business therein, such period of thirty days shall begin to run after 28

In addition to any the completion of such alterations. other damage, the cost of removal of all property shall be a lawful measure of damage. In any case where such landlord during such year shall permit the making of alterations within such space for a tenant who shall become entitled to possession after the termination of such period of one year, such liability for damages on account of dispossession shall not apply.

A lease shall be deemed or construed to be non-cancellable, within the meaning of this subdivision, even though it permits either landlord or tenant, or both, to cancel the same if (1) possession is not made available to the tenant within one year after the execution thereof; or (2) the premises are taken by the exercise of eminent domain or (3) the premises are destroyed by fire; but if a lease shall include such provision the court, before authorizing the execution of a warrant, shall require satisfactory evidence that the election to cancel the new lease has not been exercised, and in the absence of such evidence, the court shall dismiss any action or proceeding or avoid any final order, judgment, warrant or other process against the tenant in possession. 

As used in this subdivision the word "store" shall mean commercial space at street level and two floors above and below street level or any part thereof, provided it includes space at street level, used or to be used and/or occupied or to be occupied by a tenant for the sale of personal property and/or the rendition of services in the ordinary course of business. (kk) the landlord, subject to the following conditions, receives and accepts a bona fide offer to enter into a lease with a prospective tenant, including a tenant then occupying commercial space in the same building, hereinafter in this subdivision in either case referred to as the first tenant, 29

for commercial space occupied by a tenant in possession under an expired lease with less than twenty-five per centum of the net rentable area of the floor, hereinafter in this subdivision referred to as the second tenant, provided such lease is for a term of five years or more and provided the space occupied by the second tenant is included within a larger space covering the entire rentable area of at least one floor in the same building to be occupied or in part then occupied by the first tenant, and delivers to such second tenant a notice in writing to the effect that the landlord has agreed to lease to the first tenant the space occupied by the second tenant, and such second tenants fails, neglects or refuses to move within thirty days after service of such notice. In the event that the first tenant already occupies more than two times the floor space in the building occupied by the second tenant, this subdivision shall apply even though such second tenant occupies more than twentyfive per centum of the net rentable area of the floor. In the event such second tenant shall be dispossessed from such space under this subdivision, if such first tenant shall fail, after thirty days subsequent to such dispossession, to occupy such space from which the second tenant has been dispossessed, or if the landlord shall lease or rent such space to or permit occupancy thereof by a third person within a period of one year after such dispossession, the landlord shall be liable to the tenant so dispossessed for all damages sustained on account of such removal, provided, however, that if the making of alterations shall prevent the active conduct of the business of the first tenant in such space, such period of thirty days shall begin to run after the completion of such alterations. In addition to any other damage, the cost of removal of all property shall be a lawful measure of damage. In any case where such landlord during such year shall permit the making of alterations 30

within such space for a tenant who shall become entitled to possession after the termination of such period of one year, such liability for damages on account of dispossession shall not apply. (1) a person or a corporation which by assignment shall succeed or shall have succeeded to the rights of a lessee of an entire building for a term of not less than ten years covered by a lease which was executed prior to January twenty-fourth, nineteen hundred forty-five, seeks in good faith to recover possession of the commercial space for his or its own immediate and personal use or for the immediate and personal use of a subsidiary corporation at least ninety per centum of whose stock is owned by such assignee; provided, however, that nothing in this subdivision contained shall authorize the dispossession of a tenant during the term of his lease by such landlord, person or corporation, unless by the terms of the lease, the privilege is reserved to terminate the lease for reasons which would not preclude the remedy afforded under this subdivision. If the landlord, person or corporation shall fail, after thirty days subsequent to dispossessing a tenant under the provisions of this subdivision, to occupy such space and actively to conduct such business therein, or if the landlord, person or corporation shall lease or rent such space to or permit occupancy thereof by a third person within a period of one year after such dispossession, he or it shall be liable to the tenant for all damages sustained on account of such removal. In addition to any other damage, the cost of removal of plant and property shall be a lawful measure of damage. (m) Possession is sought by a person who is a main tenant and who is a practitioner of law, medicine, dentistry, veterinary medicine, optometry, podiatry, architecture, engineering or surveying, who shall have sublet all or part of his commercial space, including all or part of his profes- 31

sional equipment, who seeks in good faith to obtain possession of such space for his immediate and personal use. 

If such main tenant shall fail, after thirty days subsequent to dispossessing a subtenant under the provisions of this subdivision, to occupy such space and actively to conduct such practice therein, or if such main tenant shall sublet or rent such space to or permit occupancy thereof by a third person within a period of one year after such dispossession, such main tenant shall be liable to the subtenant for all damages sustained on account of such removal. In addition to any other damage, the cost of removal from such space shall be a lawful measure of damage.

Any person entitled to possession of real property or any part thereof under any of the provisions of this section, shall be entitled to maintain and prosecute summary proceedings pursuant to article eighty-three of the civil practice act in order to obtain such possession. 

Where a final order has been entered in any proceeding to evict a tenant pursuant to the provisions of this section, the court shall have power to stay the issuance of a warrant for a period which shall not extend beyond six months following the time of commencement of the proceeding, or two months from the entry of the final order, whichever is longer, except that where a final order has been entered pursuant to the provisions of subdivision (g) of this section the court shall have the power to stay the issuance of a warrant for a period which shall not extend beyond two months following the time of commencement of the proceeding, or one month from the entry of the final order, whichever is longer, or where any tenant in possession fails, neglects or refuses to execute a lease or be relocated within the time prescribed in subdivisions (k) and (kk) respectively of this section, the court shall have the power to stay the issuance of a warrant for a period which shall not ex- 32

tend beyond three months following the time of commencement of the proceeding, or one month from the entry of the final order, whichever is longer, but in no event to extend beyond the emergency period as defined in section eighteen. § 9. Application to continuation lease. In the case of any lease or rental agreement for use or occupancy of commercial space, which lease or rental agreement was entered into for a term commencing subsequent to the effective date of this act by a tenant who occupied such space under a prior lease or rental agreement, the expiration date of which immediately precedes the date of commencement of the subsequent lease or rental agreement, and the term of which subsequent lease or rental agreement exceeds the term of such prior lease or rental agreement, the supreme court upon action commenced by the tenant within sixty days after the effective date of this act, may abridge the term of such subsequent lease or rental agreement, as justice shall require, but in no event so that such term shall be less than that of the prior lease or rental agreement, provided that the tenant shall establish that such subsequent lease or rental agreement was, on the date of its execution, unjust, unreasonable and oppressive as to any of the terms and provisions thereof other than the rent reserved therein. 

§ 10. Exoneration of landlord; options.-No landlord shall be answerable in damages or otherwise for failure to give possession to a new tenant not in possession where the tenant in possession is permitted to hold over by virtue of any of the provisions of this act. An option in favor of a tenant to purchase, lease or otherwise to acquire any interest in the premises which option is contained in any 

-3 

33

lease or agreement shall not survive the expiration date of such lease or agreement. § 11. Rents stabilized.-Rents frozen and stabilized on 

January twenty-fourth, nineteen hundred forty-five, shall continue to be frozen and stabilized during the continuance of the emergency declared by this act. Nothing contained in this act shall create any claim or cause of action in favor of a tenant against a landlord to recover moneys paid as rent for commercial space for a period prior to the twentyfourth day of January, nineteen hundred forty-five; nor shall any cause of action in favor of a tenant against a landlord which shall have been or shall be created by this act be instituted unless begun within one year after the cause of action accrued except that in the case of any such cause of action accrued prior to April eleventh, nineteen hundred forty-nine, such cause of action shall be barred unless begun within one year from such date. § 12. Waiver; release.-Any waiver of any of the provisions of this act shall be unforceable and void, but a release by a tenant, in writing and for a valid consideration, shall not be deemed to be such a waiver. § 13. Premises exempt.-Nothing contained in this act shall be construed to apply to any hotel, rooming house, or other housing accommodations in the defense rental area defined in public document number forty-two thousand four hundred seventy-six, issued December twenty-sixth, nineteen hundred forty-four, by the office of price administration of the United States, or to any pier, dock and wharf property, theatre, motion picture house or theatre, sports arena or stadium, turkish bath or exhibition hall. The provisions of this act, likewise, shall be inapplicable to (a) 

34

any lease or rental agreement for commercial space for a term or terms aggregating not more than four months in any calendar year, to (b) any lease or rental agreement for the temporary use of land as a parking lot for motor vehicles pursuant to subdivision (cc) of section eight, and to (c) any commercial space in any building or other rental area all or part of which heretofore has been taken or hereafter shall be taken in condemnation; to (d) any commercial space with respect to which the state or any city or the New York City Housing Authority has heretofore acquired or shall hereafter acquire the status of landlord.

The provisions of this act also shall be inapplicable with respect to any commercial space now vacant or hereafter vacated by a tenant, or to space demised under a lease or sublease executed subsequent to March thirty-first, nineteen hundred fifty to a person not in possession at the time of the execution thereof or to any space included in any demise which is contiguous and in possession of the tenant at the time of the making of such lease or sublease. 

The provisions of this act shall also be inapplicable to any store or other commercial space with respect to which a lease shall be hereafter entered into between the landlord and the tenant in occupancy thereof under an expired lease or tenancy or as a monthly or statutory tenant, upon the offer of the landlord for the term and otherwise as provided in subdivision (gg) of section eight of this act, subsequent to the date of expiration of such lease, as well as to any store or other commercial space with respect to which the further period of six months has elapsed subsequent to the failure, neglect or refusal of the tenant to execute the lease offered by the landlord within the period of twenty days after such offer.

The provisions of this act shall also be inapplicable to 35

any store or any commercial space with respect to which either: (a) the tenant in occupancy thereof occupies such space as assignee or sub-lessee under an assignment or sublease made after July first, nineteen hundred fifty-nine, whether such assignment or sub-lease was made with or without the consent of the landlord and whether or not the space so occupied represents all or part of the space occupied by the assignor or sub-lessor; or (b) the tenant in occupancy thereof is a corporation controlled by principals whose control has been acquired after July first, nineteen hundred fifty-nine, from other principals and which corporation was a tenant of such premises for some period during the term of such other control, and whether or not the space so occupied represents all or part of the space occupied by said corporation during the period of such other control. § 14. Construction of act; variable or graduated rent; severability of act. To the extent that the provisions of this act are inconsistent with the provisions of any general, special or local law or charter provisions, the provisions of this act shall be controlling. 

Any lease existing or future where the specified rent or any part thereof is variable according to volume or other criteria of volume of the tenant's business shall continue without change, but where such lease provides for the payment of a fixed, basic or minimum rent, such fixed amount shall be subject to the provisions of this act including all of the provisions of this section; provided, however, that nothing in this provision contained shall be deemed or construed to bar the fixation of rent, after the expiration of any such lease, pursuant to the provisions of section four hereof. The provisions of any lease for a term of more than three years, in force since on or before 36.

March first, nineteen hundred forty-three, wherein the rental stipulated is in a graduated scale, shall determine the rents payable during the term of such lease notwithstanding the subsequent modification of any of its terms; and upon the expiration of such lease, the emergency rent shall be a reasonable rent to be fixed by agreement, by arbitration, or by the supreme court upon the basis of the rent charged for the most nearly comparable commercial space in the same building or other rental area, or other satisfactory evidence, at the time of such agreement, or on the date the application to the supreme court or submission to arbitration is made. Pending the fixation of such reasonable rent, without prejudice to their respective rights, the tenant shall pay and the landlord may accept rent upon the basis charged during the last month of the expired lease. 

If any provision of this act or the application thereof to any person or circumstance is held invalid the remainder of the act and the application of such provisions to other persons or circumstances shall not be affected thereby. § 15. Application to new construction. In order to encourage new construction and to relieve the shortage of commercial space, the provisions of this act shall be inapplicable to any new building in course of construction, or completed, on or after March thirtieth, nineteen hundred forty-six. 

§ 16. Arbitration proceedings.-The provisions of ar ticle eighty-four of the civil practice act shall apply to any controversy submitted to arbitration pursuant to the provisions of this act and any proceeding to set aside an award made by an arbitrator hereunder shall be begun within three months. Neither the oath of any arbitrator, nor any hearing provided for under such article shall be waived, 37

nor shall any person act as any such arbitrator who directly or indirectly in any way shall be connected or associated in interest or otherwise with the landlord or the tenant, and no arbitration agreement shall be submitted to the supreme court unless the application be accompanied by an affidavit of the arbitrator or arbitrators showing strict compliance with this requirement and by a transcript of the minutes of such hearing. § 17. Short title.-This act shall be known and may be cited as the emergency commercial space rent control law. 

§ 18. Duration. The emergency herein described is hereby declared to continue until July first, nineteen hundred sixty-two.

OFFICE, RETAIL STORE AND BUSINESS SPACE RENT CONTROL LAW 

(With all amendments through and including the 1961 regular session of the legislature)
